had $0 in my bank account when I decided to quit my job.\\n\\nThat one line pulls you in immediately, and that\\'s exactly the point.\\n\\nWhen I was teaching content hooks the other day, the pattern I kept seeing was people starting their posts by explaining everything before they say anything interesting. They give you the timeline, the background, the context, and by the time they get to the actual moment that matters, you\\'ve already scrolled.\\n\\nThe best storytellers skip all that and open at the most interesting moment, with stakes already baked into the first sentence.\\n\\n\"I had $0 in my bank account when I decided to quit my job\" works because the tension is right there on the first read. You feel the weight of it, and your brain immediately wants to know what happened next.\\n\\nThe weak version of that same story sounds something like, \"For a while I\\'d been unhappy at work, my coworker said something, my manager didn\\'t back me up, and eventually I just decided to leave.\" Same experience, completely different pull.
